Freud reasons that symptoms become worse for the patient in the grip of a negative therapeutic reaction because she is counter-suggestive; indications of satisfaction on the part of the analyst with the work of the analysis are met with an aggravation of symptoms. This makes it sound as if the negative therapeutic reaction is a response produced by the transference, and that all that is required is a dispassionate or disinterested attitude from the analyst. But this is a misleading impression; as Freud goes on to say, even when we allow for the attitude of defiance towards the analyst, the greater part of the analysand’s negative response remains unexplained. It is this unaccounted-for portion that Freud says contributes to an unconscious sense of guilt and associated need for punishment.

我得一开始就说明，弗洛伊德最初对负性治疗反应给出的解释，并不适用于我刚才勾勒的那种对分析产生的戏剧性、看似自发的反应。按弗洛伊德的讲法，负性治疗反应源自一种无意识罪疚感，这种罪疚感在治疗中可能以好几种方式显现，不单是随着分析推进而出现的症状恶化。弗洛伊德推论，陷入负性治疗反应的病人之所以症状会加重，是因为她具有反暗示性；分析家对分析工作流露出满意的迹象，反而会招来症状的加剧。这听上去好像负性治疗反应只是由转移催生的一种回应，似乎只要分析家摆出一副不动感情、不涉私利的态度就够了。但这是一种误导人的印象；弗洛伊德接着就说，即便我们考虑到病人对分析家的那种违抗态度，分析者负面反应里仍有很大一部分无法解释。正是这个无法解释的部分，弗洛伊德说，构成了无意识罪疚感以及与之相连的惩罚需求。

note：似乎是在说反转移。

The reason I do not think this account fits the melancholic’s response to the transference is related to the fact that, as I argue below, unconscious guilt does not offer an adequate explanation for melancholia. Because melancholia is not a malady of the superego, but a form of psychosis, the transference in melancholia is to be considered different from the negative, guilt-motivated reaction described by Freud. As we know, psychotic transference has a tendency to take on an erotomanic or persecutory dimension,4 making its handling in the treatment a rather delicate matter. The erotomanic/persecutory dimension results from the subject’s conviction that what comes from the Other takes the form “He loves me” or “He hates me.” Perhaps we can consider that the more solipsistic form of love we find in the megalomanic, “I love myself,” has an equivalent in the conviction of the melancholic, “I hate myself.”

我之所以认为这种解释不符合忧郁症患者对转移的回应，是因为——正如我下文要论证的——无意识罪疚并不能充分说明忧郁症。忧郁症不是超我的毛病，而是一种精神病的形式，因此忧郁症中的转移应当被视为不同于弗洛伊德所描述的那种负性的、由罪疚驱动的反应。我们知道，精神病性转移倾向于带上一种钟情妄想或迫害的维度，这使得在治疗中处理它变得相当微妙。钟情妄想/迫害的维度源于主体确信：来自大他者的东西采取了“他爱我”或“他恨我”的形式。也许我们可以这样想：在夸大狂身上看到的那种更唯我论的爱——“我爱我自己”，在忧郁症患者的“我恨我自己”这一确信中有着某种对等物。

There is, however, one potentially confusing point in Freud’s analysis of the negative therapeutic reaction. Freud notices a close connection between one form of the negative therapeutic reaction and melancholia: it is a form that arises from a “borrowed” unconscious sense of guilt—that is, when it is the result of an identification with someone to whom the person has been erotically attached; and it is “often the sole remaining trace of the abandoned love-relation and not at all easy to recognize as such” (Freud 1923: 50n). These remarks point to an odd, even bizarre, form of melancholia where the unconscious sense of guilt in the melancholic is the result of a direct identification with the sense of guilt carried by his abandoned love object; that is, the subject’s guilt is not a response to his own recriminations against the lost object, as in mourning, but the result of an identification with the lost object’s own superego. This is not the typical formation of a melancholic in which the libidinal tie to the object is narcissistic and we must therefore consider it a special and I think rather unusual case; it is one, moreover, with which Freud claims to have had “brilliant” success through uncovering the underlying attachment to the abandoned object.

然而，在弗洛伊德对负性治疗反应的分析中，有一个可能让人困惑的地方。弗洛伊德注意到，负性治疗反应的某一种形式与忧郁之间有着紧密的关联：这种形式源于一种“借来的”无意识罪疚感——也就是说，当这种罪疚感是主体对某个他曾情欲性地依恋过的人产生认同的结果时；而且它“常常是被抛弃的爱恋关系所留下的唯一痕迹，并且极难被认出是这么一回事”（Freud 1923: 50n）。这些论述指向了一种古怪、甚至称得上诡异的忧郁形式：忧郁者的无意识罪疚感，是直接认同于他所抛弃的爱之对象自身所携带的罪疚感的结果；也就是说，主体的罪疚并非像在哀悼中那样，是对他自己针对丧失对象的自我谴责的回应，而是认同于丧失对象自身的超我的结果。这并不是忧郁的典型构型——在典型情况下，对对象的力比多联结是自恋性的——因此我们必须将它视为一个特殊的、我认为相当罕见的案例；而且，弗洛伊德声称，通过揭示出对被抛弃对象的潜在依恋，他在这个案例上取得了“辉煌的”成功。

note：传统范式：力比多回退；这里的：通过认同，将客体的超我“摄入”自身。

It is difficult to know what more to say about these cases Freud reports. Their outcome is very much at odds with the cases particularly resistant to analytic treatment, and my inclination is to conclude that “melancholia” is not the right term to describe them. Be that as it may, they bring back the earlier question concerning the downward spiral and subsequent resistance to treatment of the type of analysand indicative, as I say, of a melancholic form of psychosis.

关于弗洛伊德报告的这些个案，很难知道还能多说些什么。它们的结果与那些特别抗拒分析治疗的个案大相径庭，我倾向于认为，“忧郁症”并不是描述它们的恰当术语。尽管如此，它们还是带回了早先的问题，即关于分析者的那种下旋以及随后的治疗阻抗，这种分析者类型，如我所说，指示着一种精神病性的忧郁形式。

“‘忧郁症’，而非‘抑郁’”（“Melancholia,” not “depression”）

Why use this term “melancholia”? It is preferable to “depression,” which is now recognized to be too diffuse and broad a term to be of clinical use. As has been noted by Michael Alan Taylor and Max Fink,

In clinical practice, depression describes a normal human emotion, a pathologic state if it is retained too long or too deeply, or a psychopathologic syndrome that may be mild or severe. A clinical depressive episode may be defined by its associated adverse life events or it may strike a subject without cause. Accepting “depression” as a medical diagnosis is equivalent to accepting “infection” as a definitive diagnostic term in clinical medicine.

为什么要用“忧郁症”这个术语？它比“抑郁”更可取，如今人们认识到“抑郁”一词太过弥散和宽泛，在临床上难以使用。正如迈克尔·艾伦·泰勒和马克斯·芬克所指出的：

在临床实践中，抑郁描述的是一种正常的人类情绪，如果持续太久或太深，则成为一种病理状态，或者是一种或轻或重的精神病理综合征。一次临床抑郁发作可能由其相关的不良生活事件来界定，也可能毫无缘由地侵袭一个主体。接受“抑郁”作为医学诊断，无异于在临床医学中接受“感染”作为一个确切的诊断术语。

“Melancholia” was already an old and familiar term by the time Kraepelin introduced it into modern psychiatry as a distinct clinical category under the name of “involutional melancholia,” though it disappeared from his system of classification in the eighth and final edition of his textbook in 1913 (Kraepelin 1913). Contemporary psychiatry has been divided over the usefulness of the term. Some have advocated that it is more precise in meaning than “depression.” Taylor and Fink, for example, describe it as a key pathology of mood characterized by a “pervasive and unremitting apprehension and gloom that colors all cognitive processes, resulting in a loss of interest, decreased concentration, poor memory, slowed thinking, feelings of failure and low self-worth, and thoughts of suicide.” It is, they say, a recurrent and debilitating condition that “alters mood, motor functions, thinking, cognition, perception and many basic physiologic processes” (Taylor and Fink 2006: 15). Then when psychoanalysis adds an even more precise understanding of melancholia, including as it does the melancholic’s reproaches, guilt and self-accusations, the vague term “depression” becomes even more unsatisfactory.

在克雷佩林将“忧郁症”以“更年期忧郁症”之名作为一个独特的临床类别引入现代精神病学之前，它早已是一个古老而熟知的术语，尽管在他1913年出版的教科书第八版即最终版里，这一类别又从他的分类系统中消失了（Kraepelin 1913）。当代精神病学对这个术语的用处一直意见不一。有些人主张，它比“抑郁”一词意义更精确。例如，泰勒和芬克将它描述为一种关键的心境病理，其特征是“一种弥漫且持续不断的忧惧与阴郁，浸染着所有认知过程，导致兴趣丧失、注意力下降、记忆力差、思维迟缓、失败感和低自我价值感，以及自杀念头”。他们说，这是一种反复发作且使人衰弱的状况，“会改变心境、运动功能、思维、认知、知觉以及许多基本的生理过程”（Taylor and Fink 2006: 15）。那么，当精神分析对忧郁症添加了更为精确的理解——包括忧郁症患者的自责、内疚和自我指控——这个模糊的术语“抑郁”就变得更加不能令人满意了。

Freud brings a structural analysis to all psychopathology, and his structural analysis of melancholia is particularly important for several reasons. First, melancholia is more than just a severe form of depression. In general, we need to be wary of the current practice of the Diagnostic and Statistical Manual for Mental Disorders (DSM) and other contemporary approaches to mental illness that diagnose according to the severity of symptoms. This wariness is particularly important where melancholia is concerned because classifying forms of depression and depressive-like states into major and minor categories on the basis of their severity and/or their duration is to assume that all have roughly the same psychopathology. This approach obfuscates the interesting questions about melancholia, because it no longer sees it as a distinct condition but rather as a phase of illness that is not structurally distinct from other depressive illnesses; it regards melancholia as lying on the same scale of severity as other depressive illnesses, as merely a depressive illness of the highest severity.

弗洛伊德对所有精神病理都做了结构分析，而他对忧郁症的结构分析尤其重要，原因有几个。首先，忧郁症不单是一种严重的抑郁形式。总的来说，我们需要对当前《精神障碍诊断与统计手册》（DSM）以及其他根据症状严重程度来诊断精神疾病的当代做法保持警惕。这种警惕在涉及忧郁症时格外重要，因为根据严重程度和/或持续时间把抑郁和类抑郁状态划分为重度和轻度类别，就等于假定它们的精神病理大致相同。这种做法模糊了关于忧郁症的那些真正值得追问的问题，因为它不再把忧郁症看作一种独特的状况，而是看作与其他抑郁性疾病在结构上没有区分的疾病阶段；它把忧郁症放在与其他抑郁性疾病相同的严重程度量表上，仅仅把它当成一种最高严重程度的抑郁性疾病。

The DSM assumes that mild and severe conditions are expressions of the same underlying condition, and its approach to depressive illnesses is no exception. One of the consequences of this—and maybe even the underlying motivation—is that in practice it often leads to the one standard treatment pattern, typically involving medication. It is recommended that for a diagnosis of depression the criteria for a major depressive disorder set out in the DSM-5 must be met (American Psychiatric Association 2013). However, the increase in categories and “specifiers” in successive editions of the DSM has tended to obscure the more fundamental distinctions, and the latest edition continues this trend. Since so called “minor depressive episodes” are frequently encountered and associated with the same negative effects as major depression, it is not surprising that there has been gradual “boundary creep.” Not only are possibly different conditions treated in the same way, but the use of antidepressants recommended only for serious depression has also gradually been extending to minor episodes as well.5 It also seems that in actual clinical practice there is an informal practice of prescribing higher doses the more severe the symptoms are, just as it is common to find antipsychotic medication prescribed for the more intractable cases of depression. Adopting different forms of treatment altogether for different forms of “depression” seems to be implicitly ruled out. It has not been lost on commentators that seeing what has come to be called “mild depression” as requiring medication is an approach that benefits the pharmaceutical industry, and yet it is well documented that a high percentage of those people who are prescribed anti depressants are not depressed by any of the standard diagnostic criteria and are therefore being prescribed a range of medications for which they are not suited.

DSM 假定，轻度和重度的状况都是同一个底层状况的表达，它对抑郁疾患的处理也不例外。这种做法的一个后果——甚至可能就是其潜在的动机——在于，实践中它常常导向单一的标准治疗模式，通常涉及药物。按照建议，要诊断抑郁症，必须满足 DSM-5 中列出的重性抑郁障碍的标准（美国精神医学学会，2013）。然而，DSM 各版中不断增加的类别和“标注”，反而模糊了那些更为根本的区分，最新版也延续了这一趋势。既然所谓的“轻度抑郁发作”频繁出现，且与重性抑郁有着同样的负面后果，那么逐渐发生“边界蔓延”也就不足为奇了。不仅可能不同的状况被用同一种方式处理，而且原本只推荐用于严重抑郁的抗抑郁药，其使用也渐渐扩展到了轻度发作。5 在实际临床中似乎还有一种非正式的做法：症状越重，处方剂量就越高，正如对更棘手的抑郁病例开具抗精神病药物也屡见不鲜。针对不同形式的“抑郁”采用全然不同的治疗方式，似乎被暗中排除了。评论者们并非没有注意到，把所谓的“轻度抑郁”视为需要药物治疗，是一种让制药业获益的做法；然而有充分文献表明，服用抗抑郁药的人当中，有很大一部分按任何标准诊断标准都不算抑郁，因此他们被开了一系列并不适合自己的药物。

Even from the perspective of biological and medication-based approaches to treatment, this flattening-out of distinctions between forms of depression, which assumes that they can all be laid out on a single spectrum from least to most severe, might account for some of their failures. For instance, there is a group of subjects diagnosed as depressed who are resistant to treatment by medication and frequently referred for electroconvulsive therapy (ECT), just as there is also a group of patients resistant to treatment by ECT itself. It would be useful to know whether there are significant differences underlying these cases of “depression.”

弗洛伊德的“哀悼与忧郁”（Freud’s “Mourning and Melancholia”）

For Freud’s structural analysis of mourning we should turn to his “Mourning and Melancholia,” which is justly considered one of his master works and a major contribution to the study of affective disorders. It is a persuasively argued text, working with a distinction between mourning, in which the subject is aware what it is that he or she has lost, and melancholia, where the loss has an unconscious source and the true nature of the loss is unknown to the subject. Freud contributes both a careful observation of the phenomenon of melancholia and a structural analysis of its origins, one that changes our views about affective disorders ranging from different forms of “depression,” as this term is now used, to whatever since de Clérambault has been known as “passionate psychosis.”6

要了解弗洛伊德对哀悼的结构分析，我们应当转向他的《哀悼与忧郁》一文，该文被公认为他的杰作之一，也是对情感障碍研究的重要贡献。这是一篇论证有力的文本，它区分了哀悼与忧郁：在哀悼中，主体清楚自己失去了什么；而在忧郁中，丧失有着无意识的根源，丧失的真实本质对主体而言是未知的。弗洛伊德既对忧郁现象做了细致的观察，也对其起源进行了结构分析，这一分析改变了我们对情感障碍的看法，涵盖范围从如今被称为“抑郁”的各种形式，直到自德·克莱朗博以来被称为“激情精神病”的种种状况。⁶

note：其实哀悼就是生活中的那种感觉，是有明确丧失对象的，而忧郁丧失的对象是向来就已经丧失的，也就是根本性的匮乏。

As masterful as Freud’s little work is, not only does it leave some unanswered questions but there are some serious problems with Freud’s analysis as well. One quite crucial problem is that the comparison and contrast he makes between mourning and melancholia is fundamentally misleading. The main difficulty is that Freud’s analysis leads to the conclusion that melancholia arises from the loss of an object to which the subject is unconsciously attached. I argue here that the opposite is true: the melancholic response arises because of the proximity of the object through its failure to have become lost. The crisis for the melancholic arises with the proximity of an invasive and what Lacan calls “ravaging” object—to use this term in its old sense of wreaking havoc—causing devastation and destruction, an object from which the subject is unable to distance himself and to which he is constantly prey. There are several advantages to regarding the object as invasive for the melancholic, and I will just mention a particularly significant one here: the object that ravages is similar to, indeed a variant of, the object that persecutes; and this is as it should be, since, as I have already said, we should regard melancholia narrowly construed as a form of psychosis.

尽管弗洛伊德这篇小作品堪称精湛，但它不仅留下了一些未解的问题，而且弗洛伊德的分析本身也存在一些严重的问题。一个相当关键的问题是，他对哀悼和忧郁症所做的比较与对照从根本上说是误导性的。主要的困难在于，弗洛伊德的分析得出的结论是，忧郁症源于主体无意识地依恋着的对象的丧失。我在此主张，事实恰恰相反：忧郁症的反应之所以产生，是因为对象由于未能成为丧失之物而过于靠近。忧郁症患者的危机，源于一个侵入性的、拉康称之为“绞缠的”对象——用这个词的古老含义即造成浩劫——所带来的靠近，这个对象造成毁坏与破坏，主体无法与之拉开距离，并不断成为它的猎物。将对象视为对忧郁症患者具有侵入性，有几个好处，我在此仅提及一个特别重要的：这个肆虐的对象，与迫害性的对象相似，实际上是其一个变体；而这正是应有之义，因为正如我已经说过的，我们应当将严格意义上的忧郁症视为精神病的一种形式。

哀悼（Mourning）

Looking in closer detail at Freud’s text, one is struck by the fact that the discussion of mourning focuses on the psychological features and almost completely ignores the ritualized aspect of mourning. Ritual is integral to the process of mourning, as the poet W. H. Auden emphasizes in his celebrated poem, written as a griever’s lament, “Funeral Blues.” The poem begins with the practice of mourning, but it soon weaves the personal grief of the griever into the description of the public, ritual nature of mourning:

Stop all the clocks, cut off the telephone,
Prevent the dog from barking with a juicy bone,
Silence the pianos and with muffled drum
Bring out the coffin, let the mourners come.
Let aeroplanes circle moaning overhead
Scribbling on the sky the message He Is Dead,
Put crêpe bows round the white necks of the public doves,
Let the traffic policemen wear black cotton gloves.

仔细审视弗洛伊德的文本，我们会发现一个引人注目的地方：关于哀悼的讨论几乎全都集中在心理特征上，而几乎完全忽略了哀悼的仪式化维度。仪式是哀悼过程不可或缺的部分，正如诗人 W. H. 奥登在他那首以哀悼者的悲叹写成的名诗《葬礼蓝调》中所强调的那样。这首诗从哀悼的实践开始，但很快就把哀悼者个人的悲痛编织进了对哀悼的公共的、仪式性质的描述之中：

停止所有钟表，切断电话，
用一块多汁的骨头阻止狗吠叫，
让钢琴和鼓声沉寂，
抬出棺材，让哀悼者前来。
让飞机在头顶盘旋哀鸣，
在天空中写出“他已逝去”的信息，
给公众的白鸽系上绉纱蝴蝶结，
让交警戴上黑色棉手套。

The ritualized practice of mourning is so prominent that the noun “mourner” does not refer to a person’s grief, or at least does so only indirectly. A “mourner” is first and foremost someone performing a ritual. She is someone who attends a funeral or who is said to be “in mourning,” frequently in a way that is prescribed either by religious law or popular custom. Mourning can mean wearing black; it can last for two weeks or forty days, where the period of mourning is frequently calculated to the day. In some cultures, it is even possible to employ professional mourners to make a public demonstration of grief to honor the deceased. In any case, the practice of mourning is less an indication of the extent of a person’s grief than a ritual that is more or less correctly carried out as a mark of respect for the deceased. Again, even where the practice of mourning is not clearly circumscribed, there are still considerations of propriety that bear on the activities associated with mourning. As Hamlet sarcastically puts it, “Thrift, thrift, Horatio! The funeral baked meats / Did coldly furnish forth the marriage tables.”7

哀悼的仪式化实践如此突出，以至于“哀悼者”这个名词指的并不是一个人的悲伤，至少不是直接指向悲伤。一个“哀悼者”首先且根本上是一个执行仪式的人。她是那个参加葬礼的人，或者那个被说成“在服丧”的人，而且服丧的方式往往由宗教律法或民间习俗所规定。哀悼可以意味着穿黑衣；它可以持续两周或四十天，服丧的期限常常精确到日。在某些文化里，甚至可以雇佣职业哭丧人来公开演示悲痛，以荣耀死者。无论如何，哀悼的实践与其说标示了一个人悲伤的程度，不如说是一种仪式，它被或多或少正确地执行，以作为对死者的尊重。再者，即便哀悼的实践没有被清晰地划定边界，仍然会有一些与哀悼活动相关的得体考量。正如哈姆雷特挖苦地说的那样：“省俭，省俭，霍拉旭！葬礼上烤好的肉，凉冰冰地就端上了婚宴的桌子。”

Why is it so important to observe the rituals of mourning? Part of the answer has to be that love for the mourned object is capable of demanding respect for the person, for his dignity. However, while love for a person can demand respect for him, it is not just a question of love; mourning is not just about one’s feelings for and affective relationship to the other but about the respect due to the person qua person. It may be true that mourning is also a mask placed over the Other that reflects back on us in inverted form the narcissism of our own survival—this is what Freud says when he writes in “Mourning and Melancholia” that the pleasure of being alive compensates for the loss of the object and thereby contributes to the overcoming of mourning. I would call this Freud’s “cynical attitude” that regards mourning as a narcissistic process in which the loss of the object is entirely overcome and leaves not a trace; the person is free to live and love again. However, there is something else going on as well. When we mourn we are not just narcissistically pained by our loss, which is the “narcissistic wound” Freud refers to elsewhere, but we also have a moral attachment—a commitment, if you will—to the memory of the person who has gone; we have a commitment to memorializing the object we have loved. While there are obvious narcissistic components to this process, the symbolic memorialization of what is lost entails leaving a record in the Symbolic of the object’s disappearance. This record is both public and private, or material and psychical. Auden’s poem subtly weaves together the narcissistic and symbolic elements, as well as the public and private aspects that are present in the mourning process.

为什么遵守哀悼的仪式如此重要？部分答案必定在于，对所哀悼对象的爱，能够要求我们尊重这个人，尊重他的尊严。然而，尽管对一个人的爱可以要求尊重他，这却不仅仅是爱的问题；哀悼不单关乎我们对他人的感受和情感关系，更关乎对这个人作为人本身应有的尊重。哀悼或许也是戴在大他者之上的一副面具，它以颠倒的形式，将我们自身幸存的自恋反射回我们身上——这正是弗洛伊德在《哀悼与忧郁》中所写的：活着的快乐补偿了对象的丧失，从而有助于克服哀悼。我会把这种看法称为弗洛伊德的“犬儒态度”，它把哀悼视为一个自恋过程，在其中对象的丧失被完全克服，不留一丝痕迹；人得以自由地去生活和再爱。然而，还有别的东西在运作。当我们哀悼时，我们不仅因丧失而感到自恋的痛苦——这正是弗洛伊德在别处提到的“自恋创伤”——我们还有一种道德上的依恋，一种对逝者记忆的承诺，如果你愿意这么说的话；我们承诺要纪念我们爱过的对象。尽管这个过程有明显的自恋成分，但对所失之物的象征界纪念化，必然要在象征界中留下对象消失的记录。这份记录既是公共的又是私人的，或者说既是物质的又是心灵的。奥登的诗微妙地将自恋元素与象征界元素，以及哀悼过程中呈现的公共面向与私人面向编织在了一起。

note：仪式还有身体以及结构性表象的因素，是外部仪式的内化，这里作者欠考虑了。

In the two quoted stanzas, Auden quite precisely brings out the narcissistic dimension of grief; a grief great enough to make the world stop and take note of the loss. Although this poem can be, and has been, seen as sentimental, to see it as nothing more than this would be to miss its subtlety. Auden himself was no sentimentalist. He is a poet who starts a poem called “Lullaby” with the lines, “Lay your sleeping head, my love, / Human on my faithless arm,” that are without illusion about the permanence of love even as he recognizes its beauty and charm. His poetry is troubled, inquisitive, uneasy, both sad and anguished, and here in this poem about mourning—or, more accurately, poem of mourning—the narcissistic aspect of grief invokes the overvaluation of the love object: “The world has lost something precious”; “Stop all the clocks”; “The breaking of so great a thing should make a greater crack”; “Let the world come to a halt.”

在那两节引用的诗行中，奥登相当精准地呈现出悲伤的自恋维度；一种巨大到足以让世界停摆、留意到这场损失的悲伤。尽管这首诗可以、也已经被视为感伤之作，但如果仅仅把它看作感伤，就会错失其微妙之处。奥登本人绝非感伤主义者。他的一首题为《摇篮曲》的诗，开篇便是：“放下你沉睡的头颅，我的爱，/ 凡人枕在我无信的臂弯，”这些诗句对爱的恒久不抱任何幻觉，即便他承认爱的美与魅力。他的诗歌充满困扰、探询、不安，既哀伤又痛苦，而在这首关于哀悼的诗里——或者更准确地说，这首哀悼之诗——悲伤的自恋面向唤起了对爱恋对象的过高估价：“世界失去了珍贵的东西”；“停下所有的钟”；“如此伟大之物的碎裂，本该发出更大的崩裂声”；“让世界就此停顿。”

I return to this topic of narcissism below. To continue with the poem, note that Auden turns away from the public to speak of his own personal grief:

He was my North, my South, my East and West,
My working week and Sunday rest,
My noon, my midnight, my talk, my song;
I thought that love would last forever: I was wrong.

Then the poem concludes with a precise depiction of the destitution of the griever who has withdrawn all interest from the world:

The stars are not wanted now: put out every one;
Pack up the moon and dismantle the sun;
Pour away the ocean and sweep up the wood;
For nothing now can ever come to any good.

我将在下文回到自恋这个话题。继续看这首诗，注意奥登从公众转向诉说自己的个人悲痛：

他曾是我的北，我的南，我的东和西，
我的工作日和星期天休息，
我的正午，我的午夜，我的言谈，我的歌；
我以为爱会永存：我错了。

然后这首诗以对哀悼者主观贫困的精确描绘作结，这位哀悼者已从世间撤回了一切投注：

现在不需要星星了：把每一颗都熄灭：
收起月亮，拆掉太阳；
倒空海洋，扫尽树林；
因为如今什么都不会再有好的结果。

Note, however, that however grief-stricken the mourner may be, however inconsolable, however painful his loss, his sense of self-worth is undiminished. He does not feel guilty. He blames the world, God, destiny, bad luck—anything—for his suffering, but scarcely holds himself to blame. Now this seems to be at odds with the psychology of mourning, one of the most prominent features of which is sometimes said to be depression; indeed, this is even acknowledged as one of the five stages of the Kübler-Ross model of grief. This is, however, merely another illustration of the shortcomings of the term “depression.” The “depression” of a griever is the depression of someone who has withdrawn her interest from the world and libidinal investment in it. This is the characteristic listlessness and introversion of the mourning subject, for whom “the stars are not wanted now” and for whom the moon can be packed away, the sun dismantled and the wood swept up. This depression is to be distinguished from the aggressive attack on the self, the remorseless punishment of the ego, which lies at the heart of the melancholic. Sartre got it wrong, at least for the melancholic: hell is not “other people”; the real hell lies in a place much closer to home, in the depths of one’s own heart, a place from which there really is no exit.

然而要注意，无论哀悼者多么悲痛欲绝，多么无法安慰，丧失多么痛苦，他的自我价值感并未减损。他不感到内疚。他责怪世界、上帝、命运、坏运气——任何东西——为他的痛苦，但几乎不责怪自己。这似乎与哀悼心理学相悖，哀悼最突出的特征之一有时被认为是抑郁；事实上，这甚至被承认是库伯勒-罗斯悲伤模型的五个阶段之一。然而，这不过是“抑郁”这个术语缺陷的又一例证。哀悼者的“抑郁”，是那种从世界撤回兴趣、撤回力比多投注的人的抑郁。这是哀悼主体特有的无精打采和内倾，对他们来说，“此刻不需要星星”，月亮可以打包收走，太阳可以拆掉，树林可以扫净。这种抑郁必须区别于对自我的攻击，那种对自我的无情惩罚，这才是忧郁症患者的核心。萨特搞错了，至少对忧郁症患者来说：地狱不是“他者”；真正的地狱在离家更近的地方，在一个人自己内心的深处，一个真的没有出口的地方。

note：作者也搞错了，地狱并不在内心深处，而是内心/外界的拓扑平面上。

Freud claims that in mourning each of the memories and expectations in which libido is bound to the object is brought up and hyper-cathected so that the libido can detach itself from it and, at the end of the process, the ego can be “free and uninhibited again” (Freud 1917a: 245).8 This is such a manifestly untrue remark that I find it curious Freud should have made it. It is obvious to the most casual observation that mourning always leaves traces behind, in the form of painful memories of a loved one, that are liable to resurface at particular moments such as anniversaries, just as they can also emerge in connection with the most unexpected things: a movie, an item of clothing, a memory of a holiday, or even with a new love. A lost object rarely disappears entirely; an object that was once loved and lost is never abandoned without leaving a trace. And yet, according to Freud, mourning involves a slow and painful process of withdrawal of one’s attachment to the memories related to the lost object and a return to the status quo ante. But even in normal mourning the lost object always casts its shadow on the ego. Even if it is true that the normal process of mourning is over when one is free of the object’s hold and free to live and love again, the ego never completely loses the mark of the object that has been lost.

弗洛伊德声称，在哀悼中，力比多与对象绑定的每一个记忆和期待都会被唤起并过度贯注，以便力比多能够从中抽离，最终使自我“重获自由与无拘”（Freud 1917a: 245）。8 这个说法明显不实，我很好奇弗洛伊德怎么会这么说。哪怕是最随意的观察也能发现，哀悼总会留下痕迹，以对逝者痛苦记忆的形式存在，这些记忆往往会在某些特定时刻——比如周年纪念日——重新浮现，同样也可能因最意想不到的事物而被触发：一部电影、一件衣物、一段假期的回忆，甚至是一段新的恋情。一个丧失的对象很少会彻底消失；一个曾经被爱过又失去的对象，从不会毫无痕迹地被抛弃。然而，按照弗洛伊德的说法，哀悼是一个缓慢而痛苦的过程，需要撤回对与丧失对象相关记忆的依恋，并回到先前的状态。但即便在正常的哀悼中，丧失的对象也总会在自我上投下它的阴影。即便正常的哀悼过程确实会在人摆脱对象的掌控、重获自由去生活和去爱时结束，自我也永远不会完全抹去那个已经丧失的对象所留下的印记。

note：这里弗洛伊德很明显是在说反升华的逻辑，但是这种尝试当然会失败。

Strangely, it took the tragic death from Spanish influenza of Freud’s fifth child, Sophie, at the age of twenty-six in 1920 for Freud to recognize that when a once loved object is lost, it is never completely abandoned and remains irreplaceable. Indeed, he recognized that the reason for this continued attachment to the object that keeps the object alive, as it were, is the very love for the object itself. On February 4 of that year he wrote to Ferenczi of his “insurmountable narcissistic insult” (Falzeder, Brabant, and Giampieri-Deutsch 2000: 7), while some years later, on April 11, 1929, in a letter consoling Ludwig Binswanger who had undergone a similar loss, Freud wrote, “We know that the acute sorrow we feel after such a loss will run its course, but also that we will remain inconsolable, and will never find a substitute. No matter what may come to take its place, even should it fill that place completely, it remains something else. And that is how it should be. It is the only way of perpetuating a love that we do not want to abandon” (Fichtner 2003: 196).

奇怪的是，直到1920年，弗洛伊德第五个孩子索菲在二十六岁因西班牙流感不幸离世，他才认识到，一个曾经爱过的对象一旦丧失，就永远不会被彻底放弃，也始终无法替代。他意识到，这种让对象仿佛继续活着的持续依恋，其原因恰恰是对对象本身的爱。那年2月4日，他在给费伦齐的信中提到了自己所受的“无法克服的自恋性侮辱”（Falzeder, Brabant, and Giampieri-Deutsch 2000: 7）。几年后，1929年4月11日，在一封安慰同样遭遇丧亲之痛的路德维希·宾斯万格的信中，弗洛伊德写道：“我们知道，这种丧失之后所感到的剧烈悲伤会慢慢过去，但我们也知道，我们将永远无法得到慰藉，永远找不到一个替代。无论什么来填补那个位置，即使它把那个位置完全填满，它也依然是别的东西。而这就该如此。这是让一份我们不愿放弃的爱得以永存的唯一方式。”（Fichtner 2003: 196）

“And that is how it should be,” writes Freud. The mourning does not and should not bring an end to the object’s presence in one’s life. Otto Kernberg comments that the process of mourning a lost object can go hand in hand with a persistent memorialization of the lost person and one’s relationship to him. As Kernberg puts it, there is not just an identification with the lost object, but also the setting up of an internal relationship with that object in its absence (Kernberg 2010: 607). It is as if out of respect for the person and one’s attachment to him, one is bent on maintaining the memory of one’s attachment to the object so that it outlives the psychical work of mourning. As Goethe once wrote, “We die twice: first when we die and then when those who knew and loved us die,” and it is as living memorials that we carry the mark of lost loved ones on our souls (as cited by Kernberg 2010).

弗洛伊德写道：“事情本就该如此。”哀悼并不会、也不应该终结对象在一个人生命中的在场。奥托·科恩伯格评论说，哀悼一个丧失对象的过程，可以与对丧失之人以及自己与他的关系的持续纪念化携手并进。正如科恩伯格所言，这里不只有对丧失对象的认同，同时还在对象缺席的情况下建立起了与它的内在关系（Kernberg 2010: 607）。仿佛是出于对那个人的尊重以及自己对他的依恋，人们执意要维持自己对对象的依恋记忆，好让这份记忆比哀悼的心理工作活得更久。歌德曾写道：“我们死两次：第一次是我们自己死去，然后是那些认识我们、爱我们的人死去。”而我们正是作为活着的纪念碑，在灵魂上携带着逝去挚爱的印记（转引自科恩伯格，2010）。

note：presence不好，对象向来是不在场的回溯建构出来的在场。

When the mourning is over, the object is still preserved in some way; and this is as it should be. As distressing as the persistent memory of a lost loved one may be, there may be no question for the person who has suffered a loss to want to forget his loss. Moreover, the sadness, the regret, and the pain experienced over a loss can be experienced without having any impact on the person’s self-regard. One’s life may be impoverished by the loss of a loved one, but without one’s sense of one’s own worth being diminished.

当哀悼结束，对象仍以某种方式被保存着；这本来就是该有的样子。对逝去爱人的记忆挥之不去，固然令人痛苦，但对于承受丧失的人来说，想要忘记他的丧失，可能根本就不是一个问题。而且，因丧失而感受到的悲伤、遗憾和痛苦，可以丝毫不影响一个人的自我评价。失去爱人或许会让生活变得贫瘠，但一个人对自身价值的感受却不会因此减损。

note：如果说自身价值的感受是自我认同的话，通过仪式性地哀悼丧失反而会加强自我认同。

In thinking about mourning it is, then, important to emphasize the push to memorializing and the respect for the dead demanded of and by the bereaved, those who loved and have, in a sense, been left behind by the death of a friend or loved one.9 The process of mourning involves a slow and painful repairing of the rent in the Symbolic produced by the subject’s loss. The rent can only be patched through the two aspects of mourning: ritual, a symbolic process that mobilizes the entirety of the Symbolic order; and the psychical work of bereavement. It is important that the two processes be interwoven; the psychical work of bereavement is better achieved when accompanied by ritual at the level of the group or the community.

在思考哀悼时，要着重强调那种对纪念的推动，以及丧亲者——那些爱过死者、并在某种意义上被朋友或爱人的死亡抛在身后的人——所要求、也被要求给予死者的尊重。哀悼的过程，涉及对主体因丧失而在象征界中撕开的裂口进行缓慢而痛苦的修补。这个裂口只能通过哀悼的两个方面来弥合：一是仪式，那是一种调动起整个象征秩序的象征过程；二是丧恸的精神工作。至关重要的是，这两个过程要交织在一起；当丧恸的精神工作有群体或共同体层面的仪式相伴时，会完成得更好。

忧郁（Melancholia）

Freud discerns two essential preconditions for melancholia: narcissistic object-choice and regression to the oral phase. The narcissistic nature of the investment explains the strong attachment to the object alongside a readiness to abandon the object when obstacles arise. The narcissistic investment means there is an unconscious withdrawal of libido from the object and its investment in the ego, which is the same process by which Freud explains the collapse of the world, or “world-catastrophe,” that occurs in the case of Schreber and other cases of paranoia (Freud 1911: 69ff).

弗洛伊德识别出忧郁症的两个基本前提：自恋性的对象选择，以及退行到口欲期。投注的自恋性质，解释了为什么对对象既有强烈的依恋，又在遇到障碍时随时准备放弃对象。自恋投注意味着力比多从对象那里无意识地撤回，转而投注到自我之中——弗洛伊德正是用这同一个过程，来解释施瑞伯案例以及其他偏执狂案例中发生的世界崩塌，即“世界灾难”（Freud 1911: 69ff）。

note：退行到口欲期——精神病结构。

This lends support to my argument that there is an underlying psychosis in melancholia, and that the negative reaction in the treatment arises from the circumstances of the transference. If this is so, then appealing to a negative therapeutic reaction is not decisive when enquiring whether the reaction is psychotic or not, given that this reaction can be present in other conditions, obsessional neurosis in particular. I also doubt whether the melancholic is suffering from a malady of the superego, as Freud believes.

这支持了我的论点：忧郁症底下潜伏着精神病，而治疗中出现的负性反应，是源于转移的种种情境。如果确实如此，那么在探究这种反应是否属于精神病性时，诉诸负性治疗反应就不是决定性的，因为这类反应也可以出现在其他状况里，尤其是强迫型神经症。我也怀疑，忧郁症患者是否真如弗洛伊德所认为的那样，正遭受着超我之疾。

While the narcissistic object-choice, which combines a strong attachment to the object with a paradoxical readiness to relinquish it, is crucial, it does not explain why the ferocity of the criticism addressed to the melancholic ego is as intense as it is—and yet, this is precisely what needs to be explained, since it is what is so specific to and so puzzling about melancholia. It may well be, as Freud holds, that attacks on the ego are really attacks on the lost object, yet we are left with no idea why these attacks should be so ferocious. We know that, with one major exception, which I come to below, the aggressive attack on the self and the remorseless punishment of the ego are specific to melancholia and are absent from mourning. For the melancholic, the loss of self-respect is the loss of the narcissistic illusion that he is a meritorious human being. The melancholic believes that it is his misfortune to be alive when being alive is a fate worse than death.

虽然自恋性对象选择——它将对对象的强烈依恋与一种悖论性的随时准备放弃对象结合在一起——至关重要，但它并不能解释为什么针对忧郁症自我的批评会如此猛烈；然而，这恰恰是需要解释的地方，因为正是这一点构成了忧郁症最特异、最令人费解之处。很可能如弗洛伊德所认为的那样，对自我的攻击实际上是对那个已丧失对象的攻击，但我们仍然完全不明白，为什么这些攻击会如此凶猛。我们知道，除了一个主要的例外（我稍后会谈到），对自身的攻击性打击和对自我的无情惩罚是忧郁症所特有的，在哀悼中并不存在。对忧郁症患者来说，自尊的丧失，就是丧失了那种自恋幻觉——以为自己是一个有价值的人。忧郁症患者相信，活着本身就是他的不幸，因为活着是一种比死亡更糟糕的命运。

Freud explains this by saying that the self-criticisms are really directed at another person. He reasons that the ego identifies with the abandoned object and is subjected to harsh and cruel punishment by the superego as if it were this object.

弗洛伊德对此的解释是，这些自我批评实际上指向的是另一个人。他推论说，自我认同于那个被抛弃的对象，并因此受到超我严厉而残酷的惩罚，就好像自我就是那个对象一样。

The withdrawal of libido from the world and its narcissistic investment in the ego is further indicated by the absence of shame and the insistence with which the melancholic repeatedly reasserts worthlessness. But the self-criticism, which is a sort of collapse of narcissism, is different. Not only is it the most dangerous, even potentially fatal, aspect, but it is also the aspect that is the most paradoxical and difficult to explain. It simply cannot be explained in terms of the ego, which is now the target of the criticisms of the lost object. Criticisms levelled at the lost object are often present in mourning, but in no way do they acquire the horrendous level of the devastating attacks on the ego.

力比多从世界撤回、转而自恋地投注到自我，这一点还体现在羞耻感的缺失，以及忧郁主体反复坚称自己毫无价值的那股执拗上。但自我批评——它可算是自恋的某种崩塌——则不同。这不单是最危险、甚至可能致命的面向，也是最悖谬、最难解释的面向。它根本没法用自我来理解，因为此时自我恰恰成了那个丧失对象所发出的批评的靶子。在哀悼中，针对丧失对象的批评常常出现，但这些批评绝不会达到对自我进行毁灭性攻击的那种骇人程度。

Freud is aware of this, which is why he adds a second dimension to the mechanism of melancholia; as he puts it, the regressive identification with the object is accompanied by a second form of regression, to sadism under the influence of ambivalence. This is fine so far as it goes, but it does look like a redescription rather than an explanation; explaining the severity of attacks on the ego by appealing to a regression to sadism is like saying sleeping pills work by virtue of their dormative powers. We are forced to conclude, then, that Freud’s explanation is inadequate, since it does not explain the ravages of melancholia as an identification that is then subject to the criticisms to which the object was previously susceptible.

弗洛伊德清楚这一点，所以他给忧郁症的机制添上了第二个维度；用他的话说，在矛盾情感的影响下，退行性地认同对象的同时，还伴随着第二种退行——退行到施虐。这么说倒也没错，但看起来更像是换了个说法，而不是解释；用退行到施虐来说明自我遭受的猛烈攻击，就好比说安眠药起作用是因为它有催眠效力。于是我们不得不认为，弗洛伊德的解释并不充分，因为它没能说明忧郁症的肆虐——这种认同随后便承受了那些原本指向对象的批评，而对象先前就很容易招来这些批评。

note：弗洛伊德解释现象学味道很重，忧郁症之所以会攻击自己，是因为他退行到了施虐。

There is another problem as well. The account in terms of regression to sadism allows for no distinction between melancholia and obsessional neurosis, where, for Freud, the depression so typical of obsessional neurosis also arises from the aggression towards the object with which the ego has identified. Therefore, if we are to distinguish between obsessional neurosis and melancholia, there must be some important transformation that takes place when the forsaken object is “internalized.”

还有一个问题。那种用退行到施虐来解释的说法，无法区分忧郁症和强迫型神经症，因为在弗洛伊德那里，强迫型神经症中极为典型的抑郁，也同样源于自我对它所认同的对象产生的攻击性。因此，如果我们要把强迫型神经症和忧郁症区分开来，那么在被抛弃的对象被“内化”的那一刻，一定发生了某种重要的转变。

I earlier described regression to the oral phase as the second of two preconditions for melancholia. Can this explain the devastation of melancholia? The question of regression to the oral stage in melancholia was taken up by Freud as a result of discussions over many years with his disciple and collaborator, Karl Abraham. The significance of the discussion is that it was clearly seen as a way to capture the very different nature of the relation to the lost object displayed by the melancholic subject. It is in this relation that Freud seeks to discover what is specific to the melancholic’s response to loss, in the hope that it will clarify why the response is so different from mourning, on the one hand, and, on the other, from what unfolds in obsessional neurosis.

我先前曾指出，退行到口欲期是忧郁症的两个前提条件中的第二个。这能解释忧郁症的那种毁灭性吗？关于忧郁症中退行到口欲阶段的问题，是弗洛伊德在与他的弟子兼合作者卡尔·亚伯拉罕经过多年讨论之后才着手处理的。这场讨论的意义在于，它显然被看作一种方式，用来把握忧郁主体所展现的与丧失对象之间那种截然不同的关系性质。正是在这种关系中，弗洛伊德试图发现忧郁者对丧失的反应究竟有何特异性，希望借此阐明，为什么这种反应一方面与哀悼如此不同，另一方面又与强迫型神经症中所展开的情形如此不同。

Having described oral eroticism in the first (1905) and second (1910) editions of the Three Essays on Sexuality, it is only in the third (1914) edition that Freud speaks of an actual oral or “cannibalistic” phase prior to the anal phase. He deepened his understanding of this phase and further elaborated it in his study of the Wolf Man, which was completed but for the appendix by November 1914. Thus, when Freud wrote the first draft of “Mourning and Melancholia” in February 1915 and finished it by May 4 that same year, he already had a detailed understanding of the oral, cannibalistic stage. Freud writes in “Mourning and Melancholia” that the oral phase is the earliest stage of object-choice and thus the first way in which the ego picks out an object; that it does so by seeking to incorporate the object into itself; and that, in accordance with the oral or cannibalistic phase of libidinal development, this means devouring it. The same view finds expression in “Instincts and Their Vicissitudes,” where Freud reiterates the view that incorporation of the object is the “preliminary stage” of love or “a type of love which is consistent with abolishing the object’s separate existence” (Freud 1915: 138).10

在《性学三论》的第一版（1905）和第二版（1910）里，弗洛伊德已经描述了口唇性欲，但直到第三版（1914），他才谈到一个真正的口欲期，或者说“食人”期，并且把它放在肛欲期之前。他对这个阶段的理解，在对狼人的研究中得到了深化和更细致的展开——这项研究到1914年11月时，除了附录部分，已经全部完成。因此，当弗洛伊德在1915年2月写下《哀悼与忧郁》的初稿，并在同年5月4日定稿时，他对口欲的、食人性的阶段已经有了很详细的把握。弗洛伊德在《哀悼与忧郁》中写道，口欲期是对象选择的最早阶段，因而也是自我挑出一个对象的最初方式；这种方式表现为自我试图把对象吞并进自身；而且，按照力比多发展的口欲期或食人期的逻辑，这就意味着把对象吞噬掉。同样的看法也出现在《冲动及其变迁》中，弗洛伊德在那里重申：对象的吞并是爱的“预备阶段”，或者说“一种与取消对象的分离存在相一致的爱”（Freud 1915: 138）。

The point of these remarks is that, while subsequent views about the oral stage such as Klein’s emphasize that oral incorporation is an aggressive and destructive attack on the object, this is not Freud’s view. Freud considered that indifference to the fate of the object would be a more accurate description of the ego’s attitude; the intention is not one of aggressive attack and destruction. This is borne out in many places, including a comment in “Instincts and Their Vicissitudes,” where Freud says that incorporation is a primitive form of love in which we can speak of unconcern or indifference but not of love and hate in the true sense of these terms (1915: 138–9).11

这些论述的要点在于：虽然后来关于口欲期的观点——比如克莱因的——强调口欲并入是对对象的一种攻击性和破坏性的袭击，但这并不是弗洛伊德的看法。弗洛伊德认为，对对象的命运漠不关心，才是对自我态度的更准确描述；其意图并非攻击与破坏。这一点在诸多地方都得到了印证，包括《冲动及其变迁》中的一段评论，弗洛伊德在那里说，并入是一种爱的原始形式，在这种形式中我们可以谈论不关心或漠不关心，却不是在真正意义上谈论爱与恨（1915: 138–9）。

note：侵凌性的爱。

Freud never considered aggressive impulses to be primary. He viewed aggression and associated murderous impulses towards the object as secondary to, and derivative of, the desire to incorporate the object, and he relates sadistic impulses specifically to obsessional neurosis, not to melancholia. When, in Introductory Lectures on Psycho-Analysis, he refers to the sadism of the obsessional, it is to point out that it is a disguised form of love for the object. The “obsessional idea ‘I should like to kill you,’” he says, “means at bottom nothing other than ‘I should like to enjoy you in love’” (Freud 1917b: 344). Time and again Freud emphasizes the erotic dimension to oral incorporation and oral fantasies. On the other hand, when murderous impulses are directed towards the Oedipal rival they are motivated by the desire for exclusive access to the love object. In no circumstance do we ever find expression given to a primary aggressive or sadistic impulse in Freud.

弗洛伊德从不认为侵凌性冲动是原初的。在他看来，指向对象的侵凌以及与之相伴的谋杀冲动，是继发于合并对象的欲望、并由此衍生出来的；而且他特别把施虐冲动与强迫神经症联系在一起，而不是与忧郁症挂钩。在《精神分析引论》中，当他提及强迫症患者的施虐时，恰恰是为了指出，那不过是对对象的爱的一种伪装形式。他说，“‘我想要杀了你’这个强迫观念，说到底无非意味着‘我想要在爱中享受你’”（Freud 1917b: 344）。弗洛伊德一再强调口腔合并与口腔幻想中的爱欲维度。另一方面，当谋杀冲动指向俄狄浦斯情敌时，其动机是想要排他性地接近那个爱的对象。在弗洛伊德那里，我们绝找不到任何原初的侵凌性冲动或施虐冲动的表达。

note：排他性的爱，口欲期。

In short, we see Freud pinpointing oral incorporation as a feature that is specific to melancholia but that does not help explain the fundamental issue: the melancholic is ravaged by an object he is unable to separate from. Freud’s claim that in melancholia the regression is to an oral, cannibalistic relationship to the internalized object12 actually goes to the heart of melancholia. Still, the ravaging nature of the relationship remains to be explained.

简而言之，我们看到弗洛伊德把口腔合并指认为忧郁症的一个特有特征，但这无助于解释根本问题：忧郁者被一个他无法与之分离的对象所绞缠。弗洛伊德声称，在忧郁症中，退行是退回到一种与内化对象的口腔性、食人式的关系，这实际上触及了忧郁症的核心。然而，这种关系的绞缠性质仍有待解释。

note：也就是这种退行的结果并非与他者的手足相争，而是对自己身体的破坏。

I will now examine some similarities and differences between melancholia and obsessional neurosis. The obsessional’s reaction to loss can resemble the ravages in melancholia, with aggressive attacks on the self and remorseless punishment of the ego that resemble melancholia quite closely. Freud attributed this feature of obsessional neurosis to the ambivalence of the obsessional’s relationship to his object. We can now pursue the comparison between melancholia and obsessional neurosis further. 13

我现在来考察一下忧郁症与强迫型神经症之间的一些相似和差异。强迫症主体对丧失的反应，可能很像忧郁症里的那种绞缠，表现为对自身的攻击性打击、对自我的无情惩罚，这些都与忧郁症相当接近。弗洛伊德把强迫型神经症的这一特征，归因于强迫症主体与其对象关系中的矛盾情感。我们现在可以进一步推进忧郁症与强迫型神经症之间的比较。13

忧郁症和强迫性神经症（Melancholia and obsessional neurosis）

First, melancholia in remission, when the melancholic is neither manic nor depressed, may have strong obsessional characteristics. It is not clear how widely this applies, and it is a matter for further enquiry. Psychiatry has not taken much interest in melancholia in this state, viewing it as simply the absence of mania and depression and in no particular need of attention. This is unfortunate, since melancholia typically has long periods of remission and these periods are also part of being a melancholic. Now, in many cases, we find numerous similarities between melancholia in remission and obsessional neurosis—so many, in fact, that if we only ever saw a melancholic in a state of remission it would be easy to take him for an obsessional. The obsessional character of the melancholic is displayed in a concern for cleanliness and order, where traits of obstinacy and defiance combine with an apparent docility and well-meaning attitude. I refer you to the description above of the melancholics one encounters clinically. One can also find, in sharp contrast with the profligacy of the manic, a frugality with money and attentiveness to personal possessions.

首先，忧郁症在缓解期——也就是忧郁症患者既不躁狂也不抑郁的时候——可能表现出很强的强迫特征。这种情况有多普遍还不清楚，有待进一步探究。精神病学对这种状态下的忧郁症没多大兴趣，只把它看作躁狂和抑郁的缺席，觉得不需要特别关注。这很可惜，因为忧郁症通常有漫长的缓解期，而这些时期同样是作为忧郁症患者的一部分。事实上，在许多个案中，我们发现缓解期的忧郁症与强迫型神经症之间有大量相似之处——相似到如果只见过缓解期的忧郁症患者，很容易就把他当成强迫型神经症。忧郁症患者的强迫性格表现在对清洁和秩序的讲究上，固执和违抗的品性与表面上的顺从、善意态度交织在一起。请参考上文对临床上遇到的忧郁症患者的描述。与躁狂者的挥霍无度形成鲜明对照，我们还能发现他们在金钱上的吝啬，以及对个人财物的悉心照管。

note：这种表述很像是试图进入神经症的倒错。。

Second, in obsessional neurosis mourning can take on a pathological aspect that is apt to turn into a kind of depression.14 As Freud writes, “Obsessional states of depression following upon the death of a loved one show us what the conflict due to ambivalence can achieve by itself when there is no regressive drawing-in of libido” (Freud 1917a: 251) such as is found in melancholia. Obsessional mourning is the result of the self-reproaches with which the obsessional blames himself for having wished the death of the object he loves. Freud subsequently points out that while the sense of guilt in the obsessional is particularly garrulous, its accusations are rejected by the ego whose protestations are not to be taken at face value, given that the sense of guilt has unconscious sources. In melancholia, on the other hand, the ego acquiesces in the accusations of guilt and submits to the punishment inflicted by the superego. The difference, Freud says, lies in the fact that in obsessional neurosis the superego’s ferocity falls on impulses foreign to the ego while in melancholia the object of its wrath is now part of the ego, which has identified with it (Freud 1917a: 51).

其次，在强迫型神经症中，哀悼可能呈现出一种病态的面向，容易转变为某种抑郁。14 正如弗洛伊德所写：“在爱人死后出现的强迫型抑郁状态向我们表明，当没有那种在忧郁症中可见的力比多退行性撤回时，由矛盾情感所导致的冲突本身就能造成什么”（Freud 1917a: 251）。强迫型哀悼是自我谴责的结果，强迫症患者因曾希望所爱对象死去而责备自己。弗洛伊德随后指出，虽然强迫症患者的罪疚感尤其喋喋不休，但它的指控被自我所拒绝，自我的抗议不能只看表面，因为罪疚感有着无意识的来源。另一方面，在忧郁症中，自我默认了罪疚的指控，并屈从于超我所施加的惩罚。弗洛伊德说，区别在于，在强迫型神经症中，超我的凶残落在与自我相异的冲动上，而在忧郁症中，其愤怒的对象如今成了自我的一部分，自我已与之认同（Freud 1917a: 51）。

In circumstances in which the self-reproaches and morbid depressive reaction to loss in the obsessional can be indistinguishable from the melancholic condition, knowing in any individual case that there has been an actual loss is diagnostically important. It is possible for melancholia to be triggered by loss, though it is not necessary, and knowing that there has been no actual loss can assist in deciding between a diagnosis of obsessional mourning or melancholia and, consequently, the risks being run in the treatment. Freud was probably correct in his assessment that obsessionals are unlikely to commit suicide, perhaps owing to the depth of ambivalence of the obsessional.

在强迫症主体那里，自我责备和对丧失的病态抑郁反应，有时跟忧郁状态难以区分，因此，知道某个具体个案中确实发生过实际的丧失，在诊断上就很重要。忧郁症有可能由丧失触发，尽管这不是必须的，而知道并没有实际的丧失，能帮助我们在强迫性哀悼和忧郁症之间做出诊断抉择，进而判断治疗中正在冒的风险。弗洛伊德的评估很可能是对的：强迫症主体不太会自杀，这或许是因为强迫症主体矛盾情感的深度。

There are clear similarities between melancholia and obsessional neurosis, but when it comes to the differences between the superego’s criticisms in obsessional neurosis and melancholia Freud’s analysis highlights the regression of the libido back into the ego in melancholia and the absence of such a process in obsessional neurosis. This indicates, as Freud says, a more narcissistic investment in the object in melancholia.

忧郁症和强迫神经症之间有明显的相似之处，但说到超我在强迫神经症和忧郁症中的批评方式有什么不同，弗洛伊德的分析着重指出，在忧郁症中力比多退行回了自我，而强迫神经症里并没有这个过程。这正如弗洛伊德所说，表明在忧郁症中，对对象的投注更偏向自恋性质。

The mention of narcissism returns us to the question of melancholia in relation to psychosis, since the withdrawal of narcissistic investment from the object onto the ego is a crucial step in the psychotic process. As we are now heading into the field of psychosis, let’s try to figure out how this works in relation to foreclosure of the Name-of-the-Father. On the assumption that melancholia is a form of psychosis, how does foreclosure operate in melancholia?

提到自恋，就把我们带回到忧郁与精神病的关系这个问题上，因为将自恋投注从对象撤回到自我，正是精神病过程中的关键一步。现在我们正进入精神病的领域，就来试着弄清楚，这一步与父之名的除权是怎样关联的。假设忧郁是精神病的一种形式，那么除权在忧郁中到底是如何运作的？

忧郁症和除权（Melancholia and foreclosure）

Freud never had a strong grasp of psychotic transference. He thought that psychotics were not suitable for analysis because they did not form a transferential bond with the analyst. We know this to be false; psychotics do form a transference, but, as discussed, one that is always liable to become erotomanic or persecutory.

弗洛伊德从未真正把握住精神病性转移。他认为精神病患者不适合做分析，因为他们无法与分析家建立起转移的联结。我们现在知道这种看法是错的；精神病患者确实会形成转移，但正如已经讨论过的那样，这种转移总是容易滑向钟情妄想或迫害妄想。

It is possible that it was Freud’s inexperience with psychotics that led to his misdiagnosis of the Wolf Man. Freud originally diagnosed Pankejeff as obsessional, which is understandable since it seems that Pankejeff was an undeclared psychotic at the time of his analysis with Freud. But by the time Freud referred Pankejeff to Ruth Mack Brunswick for a second analysis, he was paranoid if not overtly psychotic and, had Freud been more experienced with psychosis at the time of his return for treatment and subsequent analysis, he might well have detected the change in Pankejeff’s condition.15

弗洛伊德对精神病主体的经验不足，或许正是导致他误诊狼人的原因。弗洛伊德起初将潘克耶夫诊断为强迫症，这倒可以理解，因为潘克耶夫在弗洛伊德那里做分析时，似乎是一个尚未显露的精神病主体。可等到弗洛伊德把潘克耶夫转介给露丝·麦克·布伦斯维克进行第二次分析时，他已经是个偏执狂——即便还算不上明显发作的精神病。要是弗洛伊德在潘克耶夫回来接受治疗以及后续分析那阵子对精神病更有经验，他很可能就会察觉出潘克耶夫状况的变化。

Work with psychosis is one of the areas that our clinical experience has extended since Freud. Thanks to Lacan’s contribution to the psychoanalysis of psychosis and the work of those trained in the Lacanian approach, we can be confident about taking psychotic patients into analytic treatment. Lacan encouraged us “never to back away from psychosis,” not only because it was a duty but also because of what we could thereby learn.

对精神病的工作，是自弗洛伊德以来我们的临床经验得到扩展的领域之一。得益于拉康对精神病的精神分析所做的贡献，以及那些接受拉康派取向训练的分析家们的实践，我们可以有信心将精神病患者纳入分析治疗。拉康鼓励我们“绝不向精神病退却”，这不仅是因为一种责任，也是因为我们可以由此学到东西。

While Freud investigates the obvious similarities between mourning and melancholia, his analysis needs to be examined in light of the structural distinction introduced by Lacan between neurosis and psychosis if we are to understand melancholia. The reaction to the transference on the part of the melancholic indicates, as I have said, that it is the very presence of the object, rather than its loss, that is critical in the melancholic response to the transference. Freud writes that sometimes in melancholia an object has been lost, as in mourning, but that the real difference between the two is that while in melancholia who has been lost is known, what has been lost is not (Freud 1917a: 245). However, if the typical response to the transference is produced by the proximity of the object, then this suggests that melancholia is not about object loss. It follows that mourning, which is produced by the loss of an object, is a misleading model for melancholia; it might even be the case that melancholia does not arise from a loss at all, or at least, not from the kind of loss we find in mourning.

尽管弗洛伊德探讨了哀悼与忧郁症之间明显的相似性，但若要理解忧郁症，我们就需要根据拉康在神经症与精神病之间引入的结构性区分来审视他的分析。正如我所说，忧郁症患者对转移的反应表明，在忧郁症对转移的回应中，关键的是对象的在场本身，而非对象的丧失。弗洛伊德写道，有时在忧郁症中，一个对象被丧失了，就像在哀悼中一样，但两者真正的区别在于，在忧郁症中，丧失的是谁，我们是知道的，而丧失的是什么，我们却不知道（Freud 1917a: 245）。然而，如果对转移的典型反应是由对象的邻近所引发的，那么这就意味着忧郁症并非关乎对象丧失。由此可知，由对象丧失所引发的哀悼，对忧郁症而言是一个误导性的模型；甚至可能忧郁症根本就不是源于某种丧失，或者至少，不是源于我们在哀悼中所见到的那种丧失。

note：哀悼的是本在场的东西被排除了，忧郁的是对象a本身就在场，患者自己成了那个被大他者抛弃的、无价值的abject。

How are we to think about melancholia from a Lacanian perspective? To answer this question, we first need to take a step back and consider what Lacan has to say about mourning. We can begin by briefly considering an interesting comparison he makes between mourning and foreclosure (Verwerfung) in his seminar on desire and its interpretation, where he says that the death of a loved one makes “a hole in the real by means of which the subject enters into a relationship that is the inverse of … Verwerfung,” adding that this hole in the Real “sets in motion … the signifier that can only be purchased with your own flesh and your own blood, the signifier that is essentially the veiled phallus” (Lacan 1977: 37–8). The suggestion here, then, is that in mourning the loss of a loved one creates a breach in reality, thereby setting in train, in the reverse direction, the process typically involved in the triggering of a psychosis. Instead of the calamitous collapse, as in psychosis, of signifiers that reveal the absence of the Name-of-the-Father, in mourning, the loss unconsciously activates afresh phallic signifiers that libidinally bind one to objects in the world. Whereas Freud wrote that the work of mourning is a process of relinquishing the features of the object one by one, Lacan sees the process as one of the preservation of the object by constructing a memorial to it in the symbolic; in other words, the work of mourning consists of codifying imaginary features of the object, i(a), into signifiers lodged in the Other. The painful process of mourning stems from the fall of the semblants that love and desire attach us to (Lacan’s “phallic significations”); the work of mourning is the transformation of these semblants into signifiers registered in and endorsed by the Other. It is the combination of ritual as a community event (“Stop all the clocks”) with the individual’s work of mourning that achieves this commemoration. Identification with the lost object, or rather with traits of the lost object, should be seen as part of this process.

我们从拉康派的视角该如何思考忧郁症？要回答这个问题，得先退一步，看看拉康是怎么谈哀悼的。可以先简短地看一下他在《欲望及其解释》研讨班里做的一个有趣对比，他把哀悼和除权（Verwerfung）放在一起谈。他说，所爱之人的死会在实在界弄出一个“洞”，主体通过这个洞进入一种跟除权正好相反的关系，并且补充说，这个实在界的洞“启动了……那个只能用你自己的血肉换来的能指，那个本质上就是被遮蔽的菲勒斯的能指”（Lacan 1977: 37–8）。这里的意思是说，在哀悼中，丧失所爱在实在界撕开了一道缺口，从而以相反的方向，启动了通常会在精神病触发时卷入的那个过程。精神病发作时，能指系统会灾难性地崩塌，暴露出父之名的缺席；而哀悼不一样，这里的丧失会无意识地重新激活一些菲勒斯能指，这些能指用力比多的方式把人拴在世界中的对象上。弗洛伊德曾写道，哀悼工作是一个逐一放弃对象特征的过程，拉康却把这个过程看作是通过在象征界为对象建造一座纪念碑来保存它；换句话说，哀悼工作就是把对象的想象特征——也就是 i(a)——编码成坐落在大他者那里的能指。哀悼之所以痛苦，是因为那些爱和欲望把我们附着在上面的假相（拉康说的“菲勒斯意指”）垮掉了；哀悼工作就是把这些假相转化成在大他者那里登记、得到大他者背书的能指。正是仪式作为社群事件（“停掉所有的钟”）与个人的哀悼工作结合在一起，才完成了这场纪念。与丧失的对象相认同，或者更准确地说，与丧失对象的某些特征相认同，应该被看作这个过程的一部分。

note：哀悼通过仪式将对象从想象界铭刻到了象征界。

But this does not tell the whole story about mourning, which also contains a real dimension, involving the subject’s relation to the object a. In mourning, the painful loss of one’s semblants exposes the underlying object a, the cause of one’s desire and the object that one has put in place to support one’s castration.16 This is the exposure of the real of the object a, or at least a particular aspect of it, that is ordinarily hidden by the object’s ideal features.

但这并没有道尽哀悼的全部，哀悼还包含一个实在的维度，涉及主体与对象a的关系。在哀悼中，一个人假相的痛苦丧失，暴露了底层的对象a——它既是欲望的原因，也是主体放置在那里以支撑其阉割的对象。16 这就是对象a之实在的暴露，或者至少是其某个特定方面的暴露，而这个方面通常被对象的理想化特征所掩盖。

What makes melancholia so different from mourning is that the melancholic subject turns out to be defenseless against the object. The object cannot be memorialized, as in mourning, and instead remains forever there in the Real. The collapse of the semblants that otherwise veil the object persists, and the “grimace” of the object, like the grimace of a skull behind a beautiful face, is exposed; for the melancholic, the veil of semblants, the i(a), over the object a falls altogether.

忧郁症之所以与哀悼如此不同，在于忧郁的主体面对对象时毫无防御。对象无法像在哀悼中那样被铭记，而是永远滞留在实在界中。那些原本遮蔽对象的假相持续崩塌，对象的“鬼脸”——就像美丽面孔背后骷髅的鬼脸——暴露无遗；对忧郁者而言，假相的面纱，也就是覆盖在对象a之上的i(a)，彻底脱落了。

note：忧郁症患者并不是“失去了对象”，而是“作为对象而丧失了”。

How can we understand this as a psychotic process? An account might look something like this. The Symbolic order regulates imaginary jouissance, subtracting it from the subject in the process. This subtraction of jouissance, which we write as (-φ), takes place at the level of libido and the drive. Now, because in psychosis the key signifier of the Symbolic order, the Name-of-the-Father, is foreclosed, the possibility opens up of an excess of imaginary jouissance that is both unregulated and invasive. The disarray and confusion that Schreber, for instance, initially finds himself in is accompanied by invasive imaginary jouissance. Then, over the course of his psychosis, he discovers a new way of regulating jouissance, which involves constructing a new relationship to the world, a new “order of the world,” to use his phrase, which emerges with his delusional metaphor.

我们如何将此理解为一个精神病过程？一种说明大致如下。象征秩序调节着想象界的享乐，并在此过程中将其从主体那里减除。这种享乐的减除，我们写作（-φ），发生在力比多和冲动的层面。而在精神病中，由于象征秩序的关键能指——父之名——被除权，一种无调节且侵入性的想象界享乐过度的可能性便打开了。例如，施瑞伯最初陷入的混乱与困惑，就伴随着侵入性的想象界享乐。随后，在他的精神病过程中，他发现了一种调节享乐的新方式，这涉及构建一种与世界的新关系，用他的话说，一种新的“世界秩序”，这一秩序随着他的妄想隐喻而出现。

note：其实这里想象界是作为实在界的镜像出现的。

The stability of Schreber’s paranoid delusion contrasts with the earlier phase of the disorder when the excess of jouissance simply overwhelms him. In his “On a Question Prior to Any Possible Treatment of Psychosis,” Lacan accounts for this crucial, confused period of the psychosis by appealing to the “subject’s regression—a topographical, not a genetic regression—to the mirror stage,” wherein the relationship to “the specular other is reduced here to its mortal impact” (Lacan 2006: 473; emphasis added). Schreber’s voices, for instance, speak of him as a “leper corpse leading another leper corpse”; and his body is merely “an aggregate of colonies of foreign ‘nerves,’ a sort of dump for detached fragments of his persecutors’ identities” (Lacan 2006: 473). Lacan’s analysis is in terms of the distinction between the Imaginary and the Symbolic, as in Schema I, which is also cast in terms of this same Imaginary-Symbolic distinction (Lacan 2006: 476). The mortification in question is the result of the structural regression to the Imaginary relationship. The object in the Real plays no part.

施雷伯偏执妄想的稳定性，与他障碍早期阶段那种被原乐的过度完全淹没的状态形成鲜明对照。拉康在《论精神病任何可能治疗的一个先决问题》中，通过诉诸“主体的退行——一种地形学上的、而非发生学上的退行——退行至镜像阶段”来解释精神病这段关键而混乱的时期，在这一退行中，与“镜像他者的关系在此被缩减为它的致命冲击”（拉康 2006: 473；着重为原文所加）。例如，施雷伯的幻听称他为“引领另一具麻风尸体的麻风尸体”；而他的身体仅仅是“外来‘神经’殖民地的聚合体，一个堆放其迫害者身份脱落碎片的垃圾场”（拉康 2006: 473）。拉康的分析依据的是想象界与象征界的区分，就像在图式I中那样，该图式同样也是依据这种想象界与象征界的区分来构建的（拉康 2006: 476）。这里所说的僵化，是结构性地退行到想象关系的结果。实在界的对象在其中没有起到任何作用。

It does not seem unreasonable to speculate that if Lacan had written this text after 1964 instead of in 1958, he would have invoked the role object a has to play. He would have referred the mortiferous role to the object, not to a regression to the Imaginary. And he would have distinguished between the collapse of phallic signification, “Φo” in Schema I, and the unmediated presence of object a with respect to the relationship to the object. In a 1967 address by Lacan to a meeting of psychiatrists at the Sainte Anne Hospital, as part of a series of lectures under the auspices of his friend Henri Ey, we find this comment: “It is the free men, the truly free men, who are mad. There is no demand for the petit a, he holds it, it is what he calls his voices, for instance…. He does not cling to the locus of the Other, the big Other, through the object a, he has it at his disposal…. Let’s say that he has his cause in his pocket, and that is why he is mad” (Lacan 1967).17 What we should note is that the proximity of the object a in psychosis means that the subject has not separated himself from it as the object cause of desire. This separation, which for the neurotic subject is produced by the Other as locus of speech and language, both regulates and limits his jouissance. In the absence of this separation a plenitude of jouissance is apparent in such typical psychotic formations as erotomania, hypochondriasis, and the persecutions characteristic of paranoia. One should also mention the feminization of the psychotic subject, or what Lacan calls the “pousse-à-la-femme” we find in many transsexuals, with their unbridgeable certainty and sometimes persistent pursuit of surgical interventions to more closely resemble their particular ideal of femininity.

我们不妨这样推测：如果拉康是在1964年之后而非1958年写下这篇文本，他大概会援引对象a所扮演的角色。他会把那种致死的角色归于对象，而不是归于向想象界的退行。并且他会区分菲勒斯意指的崩塌——即图式I中的“Φo”——与在对象关系中对象a的无中介在场。在1967年拉康于圣安娜医院的精神科医生会议上的一次演讲中，作为其友人亨利·埃伊主持的系列讲座的一部分，我们读到这样的评论：“是自由人，真正自由的人，才是疯狂的。他对小a没有要求，他持有它，这就是他所谓的他的声音，例如……他并不通过对象a去依附大他者的位点，他把它握在自己手中……我们说他把他的原因揣在兜里，正因如此他才是疯狂的”（拉康，1967）。17 我们应当注意的是，在精神病中对象a的切近意味着主体并未将自己与作为欲望原因的对象a分离开来。对于神经症主体而言，这种分离是由作为言语和语言位点的大他者所产生，既调节也限制了他的享乐。在缺乏这种分离的情况下，享乐的满溢显现于诸如钟情妄想、疑病症以及偏执狂特有的迫害等典型的精神病构型中。还应提到精神病主体的女性化，或者拉康所谓的我们在许多跨性别者身上发现的“推向女人”，他们带着不可动摇的确定性，有时持续追求外科干预，以更接近他们特定的女性特质理想。

In melancholia we encounter the same failure of separation from the object. The depressive function is explained by the fact that the unseparated-off object, in being a “piece of the Real” (“un bout de réel,” as Lacan says) leaves the subject exposed and defenseless to its ravages. A comparison with paranoia might help: the paranoiac is prey to the evil Other who wishes him ill; the melancholic is likewise defenseless against the Real of a horrific object, unmediated by the Symbolic.

在忧郁症中，我们遭遇到的是与对象分离的同样失败。抑郁功能可以从这一点得到解释：那个未被分离掉的对象，作为“实在界的一块”（拉康所说的“un bout de réel”），让主体毫无防御地暴露在它的绞缠之下。与偏执狂做个比较或许能说明问题：偏执狂是那个盼着他倒霉的邪恶大他者的猎物；而忧郁症患者同样毫无防御地面对一个恐怖对象的实在界，且没有象征界的中介。

Let me illustrate the function of object a in melancholia with some observations concerning the case of the French author and playwright, Jean Genet.18 Genet tried to take his own life on at least one occasion and also went through some serious periods vaguely described as “depressive.” But one does not have to look very closely to detect an intimate relationship to the abject. Guéguen states that one of the paradoxes of Genet’s life was the recognition given him, “a subject who presented himself as effaced and marginal,” by “highly visible anti-authoritarian writers such as Cocteau, Sartre, Simone de Beauvoir and others” (Guéguen 2009: 101). In his biography of Genet, Sartre rightly came to attribute an existential role to “abjection” in his life, and Sartre described this abjection “as a methodical conversion, like Cartesian doubt and Husserlian epoche: it establishes the world as a closed system which consciousness regards from without, in the manner of divine understanding” (Sartre 2012: 141).

让我用关于法国作家兼剧作家让·热内（Jean Genet）案例的一些观察，来说明对象a在忧郁症中的功能。¹⁸ 热内至少有一次试图结束自己的生命，也经历过一些被含糊描述为“抑郁”的严重时期。但无需细看就能察觉，他与卑贱物之间有着一种亲密的关系。盖冈指出，热内生命中的一个悖论在于，他这样一个“呈现为被抹除和边缘化的主体”，却得到了“像科克托、萨特、西蒙娜·德·波伏娃等极为显眼的反权威作家们的承认”（Guéguen 2009: 101）。在热内的传记中，萨特恰切地将一种存在论的角色归于“卑贱”在他生命中的位置，并描述这种卑贱“是一种方法性的转换，如同笛卡尔式的怀疑和胡塞尔式的悬置：它把世界建立为一个封闭的系统，意识从外部观看这个系统，以神圣知性的方式”（Sartre 2012: 141）。

Guéguen persuasively argues that whereas in his writing Genet tries to elevate waste to the level of an agalma, his homosexuality would constantly place him in a position of detritus, of reject—homosexuality described in Edmund White’s biography as “a curse, or worse as a sentence that could not be lifted.” This identification with waste, this object a as a piece of shit, returns constantly in an aesthetic that, one might say, reveals the turd behind every object of beauty. As the title of one of his writings eloquently puts it, “What Remains of a Rembrandt Torn into Little Pieces and Flushed down the Crapper.” Or, more existentially, he wrote of himself that “something that seemed rotten to me was turning my entire previous view of the world gangrenous” (Genet 1968: 21, my translation).

盖冈颇有说服力地指出，热内在写作中试图把废物抬举到“agalma”的层面，可他的同性恋却不断把他钉在残渣、被排斥物的位置上——埃德蒙·怀特的传记把同性恋描述成“一种诅咒，甚至更糟，像一个永远无法撤销的判决”。这种对废物的认同，这种作为一块屎的对象a，在他的一种美学里反复回返，我们甚至可以说，这种美学把每一件美的事物背后的粪块都揭露了出来。正如他一篇文章的标题所直白道出的：“一幅伦勃朗的画被撕成小碎片冲下马桶后还剩下什么。”或者，更带存在意味地，他这样写到自己：“某种在我看来腐烂的东西，正把我先前整个世界观都弄得坏疽化”（热内 1968: 21，我翻译）。

My suggestion is not that Genet was melancholic, necessarily; rather, his life and literature can be seen as a battle with an invasive Real through his identification with the object a. My proposition is that in other, more clearly melancholic cases, the real presence of object a is the source of those features so characteristic of melancholia.

Having set out to understand a certain type of subject that we encounter in our clinical practice, the “melancholic,” and having looked for ways to explain their response to analytic treatment, I have argued that we can best understand melancholia as a sub-type of psychosis.

我的看法倒不是说热内一定就是个忧郁症患者；不如说，他的生命和文学可以被看作一场与侵入性的实在界的搏斗，而这场搏斗是通过他对对象a的认同来进行的。我的主张是，在另一些更明显属于忧郁症的个案里，对象a的实在在场，正是那些忧郁症特有特征的来源。
为了理解我们在临床实践中遇到的某一类主体，也就是“忧郁症患者”，并试图解释他们对分析治疗的反应，我论证说，我们最好把忧郁症理解为精神病的一个亚型。

Lacan’s structural account of the difference between neurosis and psychosis has been essential to my approach, even though Lacan talks of melancholia very rarely and does not offer much of an account of affective or passional psychoses, despite Clérambault’s attention to it. I have sketched out a way of understanding melancholia based on the idea that while the neurotic’s access to any libidinal object is via its semblants, in melancholia the veil of semblants falls from the object, leaving the melancholic exposed to the object in the real and at its mercy. This object can be either persecutory, as it is in paranoia, or abject-making, as in melancholia. Thus, Freud’s approach, which proceeds by comparing and contrasting mourning and melancholia, is at best misleading. The melancholic suffers not from eternal mourning but from an inescapable proximity to the object in the Real. I have focused on the depressive aspect of melancholia, but there is the expectation that we may now be able to understand other phenomena as well. For instance, it may be possible to regard the presence of anxiety in melancholia as resulting from an encounter with the real of the object. Another line of exploration would pursue further the distinction touched on here between the two different modes of presence of the object: the phallic presentation based on the object and its semblants, on the one hand, and on the other the object in the Real, where the encounter with the real object is at stake in melancholia.

拉康对神经症与精神病之差异的结构性阐述，对我的方法至关重要，尽管拉康极少谈论忧郁症，对情感性或激情性精神病也着墨不多，即便克莱朗博对此有所关注。我勾勒出一种理解忧郁症的路径，其基础观念是：神经症主体通往任何力比多对象的途径都是经由该对象的假相，而在忧郁症中，假相的面纱从对象上脱落，使得忧郁者暴露于实在界中的对象，任其摆布。这个对象可以是迫害性的，如在偏执狂中那样，也可以是制造卑贱的，如在忧郁症中那样。因此，弗洛伊德那种通过比较和对照哀悼与忧郁症来展开的方法，往好里说是误导性的。忧郁者所苦的并非无尽的哀悼，而是与实在界中的对象之间无法逃脱的切近。我聚焦于忧郁症的抑郁面向，但可以期待我们现在也能理解其他现象。例如，或许可以将忧郁症中焦虑的在场视为与对象的实在相遇的结果。另一条探索线索将进一步推进此处触及的区分：对象在场的两种不同模式——一方面是建立在对象及其假相之上的菲勒斯呈现，另一方面是实在界中的对象，在忧郁症中，与实在对象的相遇才是关键所在。

第十一章：疯狂、主体性和镜子阶段：拉康和梅洛庞蒂（MADNESS, SUBJECTIVITY, AND THE MIRROR STAGE：Jasper Feyaerts and Stijn Vanheule）——Jasper Feyaerts and Stijn Vanheule

介绍（Introduction）

In his 1949 essay on The Mirror Stage as Formative of the I Function, Lacan discusses the function of the ego within psychoanalytic experience. Here, he holds that “the subject’s capture by his situation gives us the most general formulation of madness—the kind found within the asylum walls as well as the kind that deafens the world with its sound and fury” (Lacan 2006b: 80). In a similar way, in his Phenomenology of Perception from 1945, Merleau-Ponty considers pathological subjectivity as “a loss of plasticity” (Merleau-Ponty 2002: 151) in the subject’s intention to renew its perceptual field. Moreover, both Lacan and Merleau-Ponty contend that the “capture” or “loss of plasticity,” which are distinctive for madness, reveal something of the nature of human subjectivity as such.

在1949年那篇关于《镜像阶段作为我功能的形成》的文章里，拉康讨论了精神分析经验中自我的功能。他在文中主张：“主体被其处境所捕获，这给了我们最一般的疯狂公式——既包括精神病院高墙内的那种疯狂，也包括以其喧嚣与愤怒震聋世界的那种疯狂”（Lacan 2006b: 80）。类似地，梅洛-庞蒂在他1945年的《知觉现象学》中，将病态主体性视为主体在更新其知觉场的意向中出现的“可塑性的丧失”（Merleau-Ponty 2002: 151）。此外，拉康和梅洛-庞蒂都认为，这种标志着疯狂的“捕获”或“可塑性的丧失”，恰恰揭示出人类主体性本身性质的某些东西。

In this chapter we discuss the intimate relation between subjectivity and madness as treated in Lacan’s early writings1 and in Merleau-Ponty’s seminal work the Phenomenology of Perception. In doing so, we outline the difference between Lacan’s and Merleau-Ponty’s readings of Henry Wallon’s psychogenetic model of the mirror stage. Both authors took a special interest in this model because of its potential to clarify their own theoretical positions: whereas for Merleau-Ponty the mirror image is an illusion that needs to be reduced to lived experience, Lacan considers the specular image non-reducible, and as formative for the experience of subjectivity. We reframe this difference in terms of the above stated convergence between the two authors on the status of madness vis-à-vis subjectivity. This brings us to the conclusion that the point of difference separating Lacan’s and Merleau-Ponty’s viewpoints on the relation between madness and subjectivity is related to the function they ascribe to misrecognition in the formation of subjectivity, as well as to the subsequent possibility (Merleau-Ponty) or impossibility (Lacan) of overcoming this misrecognition.

本章我们将讨论拉康早期著作¹和梅洛-庞蒂的开创性作品《知觉现象学》中所处理的主体性与疯狂之间的紧密关系。借此，我们将勾勒出拉康与梅洛-庞蒂对亨利·瓦隆关于镜像阶段的心理发生学模型的不同解读。两位作者都对此模型特别感兴趣，因为它有可能澄清他们各自的理论立场：对梅洛-庞蒂而言，镜像是一种需要被还原到活生生经验的幻觉，而拉康则认为镜像不可还原，并且对主体性经验具有构成性作用。我们根据上述两位作者在疯狂相对于主体性的地位问题上的一致之处，重新框定这一差异。这使我们得出如下结论：区分拉康与梅洛-庞蒂在疯狂与主体性关系上观点的关键差异，与他们赋予误认在主体性形成中的功能有关，也与随后克服这种误认的可能性（梅洛-庞蒂）或不可能性（拉康）有关。

疯狂的隐喻（The metaphysics of madness）

Before addressing Lacan’s and Merleau-Ponty’s appropriations of Wallon’s theory of the mirror stage, it will be helpful to turn to Lacan’s paper Presentation on Psychical Causality, dating from 1947. This text presents a remarkable similarity to the thread followed by Merleau-Ponty in his Phenomenology of Perception. In the same vein as Merleau-Ponty’s phenomenological critique of science’s naturalistic attitude,2 Lacan takes issue with the “organicist theory of madness” of French psychiatrist Henry Ey, who, according to Lacan, “cannot relate the genesis of mental problems […] to anything but the play of systems constituted in the material substance (l’étendue) located within the body’s integument” (Lacan 2006a: 124). Such play “always rests in the final analysis on molecular interaction of the partes-extra-partes, material-substance type that classical physics is based on” (Lacan 2006a: 124). In making this critique, Lacan confirms the phenomenological refutation of the naturalistic attitude, which assumes that madness, qua natural kind, is a determined object among other objects in the world. He proposes that instead of yet another mechanistic biological model, a theory of psychic causality is required, one that actually transcends the particular issue of madness:

在进入拉康和梅洛-庞蒂各自对瓦隆镜像阶段理论的借用之前，先看看拉康1947年的文章《论精神因果性》会很有帮助。这篇文章与梅洛-庞蒂在《知觉现象学》中所展开的思路有着惊人的相似。和梅洛-庞蒂对科学自然主义态度的现象学批判同出一脉，拉康也对法国精神病学家亨利·埃伊的“疯狂的有机论理论”提出了批评。按照拉康的说法，埃伊“只能把精神问题的发生……归结为在身体包膜之内的物质实体（广延）中所构成的各种系统的运作”（Lacan 2006a: 124）。而这类运作“说到底，总是依赖于古典物理学所基于的那种物质实体类型，即各个部分外在于其他部分的分子间相互作用”（Lacan 2006a: 124）。通过这样的批评，拉康实际上肯定了对自然主义态度的现象学反驳，这种态度假定疯狂是一种自然种类，是世界众多对象中一个已被确定了性质的对象。拉康提出，我们需要的不是再弄出一个机械论的生物学模型，而是需要一种精神因果性理论，一种真正超越疯狂这个特定问题的理论：

The problem that madness thus kindles in us owing to its pathos provides a first answer to the question I raised about the human value of the phenomenon of madness. And its metaphysical import is revealed in the fact that it is inseparable from the problem of signification for being in general—that is the problem of language for man.

疯狂因其悲怆在我们身上激起的难题，为我先前提出的那个关于疯狂现象之人类价值的问题提供了第一个回答。而它形而上学层面的意涵就显现在这个事实上：它与一般存在的意指问题无法分开——而这正是人的语言问题。

Far from reducing psychical causality to a fortuitous curiosity, Lacan stresses its metaphysical dimension. The existence of madness illuminates the nature of subjectivity as such. More particularly, madness is bound up in signification, and points towards the problem language poses for man. Language enables the human being to fictionalize reality and to live it through as a problem of truth, believed in conditionally. Lacan contends that “[t]he madman believes he is different than he is” (Lacan 2006a: 139), but in a decisive way the same holds for “the king who really thinks he is a king” (Lacan 2006a: 139). This split in subjectivity between “being” and “believing” takes us to the heart of subjectivity itself. Rather than viewing madness as an expression of biological frailty, or as a deplorable adversity that goes against true human subjectivity, Lacan holds that madness is the “permanent virtuality of a gap opened in his essence. And far from being an ‘insult’ to freedom, madness is freedom’s most faithful companion, following its every move like a shadow” (Lacan 2006a: 144). Madness is a permanent possibility for human subjectivity and this is what makes up its metaphysical significance. Instead of being an aberration, subjectivity cannot be genuinely comprehended, and would not be what it is, without madness as the limit of its freedom. We return to this idea later in the text.

拉康远非把心理因果性化约为一种偶然的奇思妙想，他着重强调其形而上的维度。疯狂的存在，照亮了主体性自身的性质。更具体地说，疯狂与意指过程紧密相连，并指向语言为人所设下的难题。语言让人类得以将现实虚构化，并将这现实当作一个真理问题来经历，一个人们有条件地相信着的真理。拉康主张，“疯子相信他与他所是的不同”（Lacan 2006a: 139），但在决定性的意义上，这一点同样适用于“那位真的以为自己是国王的国王”（Lacan 2006a: 139）。这种在“所是”与“相信”之间的主体性分裂，将我们带到了主体性的核心。拉康并不把疯狂视为生物学脆弱性的表现，或一种与真正的人类主体性相背离的可悲不幸，他坚持认为，疯狂是“在他本质中敞开的一个缺口的永久潜在性。疯狂远非对自由的‘侮辱’，它恰恰是自由最忠实的伴侣，如影随形地追随着自由的每一个举动”（Lacan 2006a: 144）。疯狂是人类主体性的一种恒久可能，而正是这一点构成了它的形而上学分量。主体性绝不是一种偏差；倘若没有疯狂作为其自由的界限，主体性就无法被真切地理解，也不会成为它当下的样子。我们将在后文中回到这一观念。

In 1947, Lacan relates psychical causality to the concept of the “imago.” Subjectivity takes shape as a “more or less typical series of ideal identifications” (Lacan 2006a: 145) start to define “who one is.” These successive imaginary identifications, taken as an ever-developing ensemble, constitute the ego. More precisely, the “imago” is the specular image by which the ego of the subject is constituted through identification, without thereby conflating the actual being of the subject with this image. It is the image by which man believes himself a man, and by which the madman believes himself other than what he is. The imago is the image by which man recognizes himself (as an image) while at the same time misrecognizing his being.3 In other words, at the basis of both madness and human subjectivity the same kind of identification can be found. This is why madness partakes of the metaphysical condition of the subject and should be comprehended in terms of the problematic relation between being and believing as such.

1947年，拉康将心理因果性与“意象”这个概念联系起来。主体性开始成形，此时“一系列或多或少具有典型性的理想认同”开始界定“一个人是谁”。这些前后相继的想象性认同，作为一个不断展开的集合，构成了自我。更确切地说，“意象”是那面镜中的形象，主体通过认同这一形象而构成自我，却并不因此就把主体的实际存在与这个形象混为一谈。人正是通过这一形象相信自己是一个人，而疯子也正是通过这一形象相信自己不是他所是的样子。意象就是人借以认出自己（作为形象）的那一形象，却同时又在这一认出中误认了他的存在。换句话说，在疯狂和人类主体性这两者的根基处，都可以发现同一种认同。这就是为什么疯狂分担着主体的形而上学状况，并且应当从存在与相信本身这组疑难关系的角度来加以领会。

note：这里应该是理想自我。

Similarly, Merleau-Ponty (1945) fulminates against reducing madness to the mere outcome of numerous causal factors that would determine its make-up. Rather than placing madness beyond human existence, he believes that madness reflects a state of subjectivity that can be comprehended in its own right:

There can be no question of simply transferring to the normal person what the deficient one lacks and is trying to recover. Illness […] is a complete form of existence and the procedures which it employs to replace normal functions which have been destroyed are equally pathological phenomena. It is impossible to deduce the normal from the pathological, deficiencies from the substitutive functions, by a mere change of the sign. We must take substitutions as substitutions, as allusions to some fundamental function that they are striving to make good, and the direct image of which they fail to furnish. The genuine inductive method is not a “differential method”; it consists in correctly reading phenomena, in grasping their meaning, that is, in treating them as modalities and variations of the subject’s total being.

同样地，梅洛-庞蒂（1945）极力反对把疯狂化约为仅仅是决定其构成的众多因果要素的产物。他不将疯狂置于人的存在之外，而是认为疯狂反映了一种就其本身即可被理解的主体性状态：

绝不能轻易把有缺陷者所缺乏并试图恢复的东西，径直转移给正常人。疾病[…]是一种完整的存在形式，它用来替代已被摧毁的正常功能的那些程序，同样也是病理学现象。我们不可能通过一个简单的符号变化，就由病态推演出正常、由缺失推演出替代功能。我们必须把替代当作替代本身来对待，视其为对某种根本功能的暗指，该替代竭力要弥补这一功能，却无法提供其直接的图像。真正的归纳法并非一种“差异法”；它在于正确地阅读现象，在于把握其意义，也就是说，把它们当作主体之整体存在的各种样态与变奏来对待。

For Merleau-Ponty, madness reflects a “modality or variation” that concerns “the subject’s vital area,” as an expression of “the subject’s total being” (Merleau-Ponty 2002: 123–4), which should be revealed through phenomenological analysis.

对于梅洛-庞蒂而言，疯狂体现出一种关乎“主体的生命区域”的“模态或变奏”，作为“主体的整体存在”之表达（梅洛-庞蒂 2002：123–4），这应当通过现象学分析来揭示。

Interestingly, Lacan explicitly addresses this phenomenological analysis in his 1947 paper. More specifically, he revisits Merleau-Ponty’s point that lived experience needs to be considered prior to any objectification and even prior to any reflexive analysis that interweaves objectification with experience. As Lacan contends:

有趣的是，拉康在他1947年的论文中明确探讨了这一现象学分析。更具体地说，他回过来重提梅洛-庞蒂的论点：活生生的经验必须先于任何对象化，甚至先于任何将对象化和经验相互编织在一起的反思性分析来被考量。正如拉康所坚持的：

For Merleau-Ponty’s work decisively demonstrates that any healthy phenomenology, that of perception, for instance, requires us to consider lived experience prior to any objectification […]. Let me explain what I mean: the slightest visual illusion proves to force itself upon us experientially before detailed, piecemeal observation of the figure corrects it; it is the latter that allows us to objectify the so-called real form. Reflection makes us recognize in this form the a priori category of extension [l’étendue], the property of which is precisely to present itself partes-extra-partes, but it is still the illusion in itself that gives us the gestalt action that is psychology’s true object here.

梅洛-庞蒂的工作决定性地表明，任何健全的现象学——比如知觉现象学——都要求我们在进行任何对象化之前，先去考虑活生生的经验。……让我解释一下我的意思：最细微的视觉错觉都证明了，在细致地、一块块地观察图形从而纠正错觉之前，错觉已经在经验的层面上强行施加给了我们；正是后一种观察才让我们得以将所谓的真实形式对象化。反思使我们在这一形式中辨认出“广延”这个先天范畴，广延的特性恰恰就是以“部分外在于部分”的方式呈现自身，然而，仍然是错觉本身为我们给出了那种格式塔作用，这个作用在这里才是心理学的真正对象。

note：格式塔不是理智在客观对象上贴上的标签，而是身体-主体与世界打交道时，自发涌现出来的结构。

However, Lacan’s recourse to Merleau-Ponty’s phenomenological maxim is highly ambiguous. After all, Lacan only focuses on the lived experience of the mirror image, and not on the lived experience of perception in its pre-objective dimension, which is what interests Merleau-Ponty. Before turning to our discussion of Lacan’s and Merleau-Ponty’s respective readings of this mirror stage, we present Merleau-Ponty’s analysis of lived experience and his critique of the naturalistic attitude.

然而，拉康对梅洛-庞蒂那条现象学箴言的援引，其实是相当暧昧的。毕竟，拉康只盯住了镜像的活生生经验，而不是知觉在其前对象维度上的活生生经验——后者才是梅洛-庞蒂真正关心的东西。在转入讨论拉康与梅洛-庞蒂各自对镜像阶段的解读之前，我们先来给出梅洛-庞蒂对活生生经验的分析，以及他对自然主义态度的批判。

自然主义态度与亲历体验（Naturalistic attitude and lived experience）

As indicated by Lacan, a principle guiding Merleau-Ponty’s phenomenological account of perception is that of uncovering the unreflective ground of lived experience, which is both presupposed and forgotten within the naturalistic attitude. For Merleau-Ponty, the first task of a genuine philosophy is the phenomenological critique of this attitude, which tends to ignore the subjective ground on which it operates. The naturalistic attitude obfuscates the constitutive role subjectivity plays in perception and sees the world as an already constituted entity that hence appears as a whole of ready-made objects. Merleau-Ponty thoughtfully summarizes the logic of this naturalistic repression:

Obsessed with being, and forgetful of the perspectivism of my experience, I henceforth treat it is as an object and deduce it from a relationship between objects. I regard my body, which is my point of view upon the world, as one of the objects of that world. My recent awareness of my gaze as a means of knowledge I now repress, and treat my eyes as a bit of matter. They then take their place in the same objective space in which I am trying to situate the external object and I believe that I am producing the perceived perspective by the projection of the objects on my retina. In the same way I treat my own perceptual history as a result of my relationships with the objective world; my present, which is my point of view on time, becomes one moment of time among all others, my duration a reflection or abstract aspect of universal time, as my body is a mode of objective space.

如拉康所指出的那般，梅洛-庞蒂对知觉的现象学阐述里，有一条原则是去揭示活生生经验的那个未被反思的基底——这个基底在自然主义态度下既被预设，却又被遗忘。对梅洛-庞蒂而言，一种真正的哲学，其首要任务就是对这种态度进行现象学批判，因为自然主义态度总是忽视它自己得以运作的那个主体性基底。自然主义态度掩盖了主体在知觉中扮演的构成性角色，把世界看成是一个已经构成好了的实体，于是世界就像一堆现成的物件那样呈现出来。梅洛-庞蒂仔细地概括了这种自然主义式压抑的逻辑：

我被存在所困，又把我经验中的视角性忘得一干二净，于是我就把经验也当成一个对象，从诸对象之间的关系来推演它。我把自己的身体——它本是我投向世界的一个视点——看作是世界中的某个对象。刚才我才觉察到自己的凝视是一种认知途径，转眼我就把它压抑下去，只把眼睛当作一些物质颗粒。它们随即在客观空间里各居其位，而我正试图把外部对象安放在这同一个空间中；我还以为，我所知觉到的那个视角，是由对象在我的视网膜上投影而成的。同样，我也把自己知觉的历史当成了我与客观世界之间关系的结果；我的当下，本是我投向时间的一个视点，却变成一切时间中的一个时刻，我的绵延只是普遍时间的一个映影或抽象面相，恰如我的身体不过是客观空间的一种样态。

note：这里有很深的海德格尔的味道。

As this passage indicates, Merleau-Ponty suggests that the body determines our view of the world, which leads him to elaborate a bodily phenomenology. In this way, Merleau-Ponty attempts to acquire knowledge of how things are genuinely experienced when the veil of the naturalistic forgetfulness of experience is dissolved. This return to the things themselves is “a return to the world which precedes knowledge, of which knowledge always speaks, and in relation to which every scientific schematization is an abstract and derivative sign-language—as is geography in relation to the country-side in which we have learnt beforehand what a forest, a prairie or river is” (Merleau-Ponty 2002: x). Such pre-predicative knowledge of lived experience can be gained through the body. The naturalistic attitude, by contrast, represses embodied subjectivity.4 As a consequence of this repression, the body is hence treated as a desubjectified object among other objects, owing nothing to the experience of the world. Therefore, Merleau-Ponty’s phenomenological analysis aims at lifting naturalistic repression, which should enable the pre-objective, lived experience to come to the fore.
